# C7-SPEC — Commit 7, the house-bot console (ADJUDICATED DRAFT)

## §0 Authority

**Order, highest first:** owner ruling **D19** (nothing about house bots reaches a player or the holder) > owner ruling **D20** (a house bot is an ordinary player in every report; every admin-only house tool is dropped) > **ruling 266** (money on the console only as usage against a configured limit) > **ruling 259/260** (a layout changes what is PAINTED, not what is SENT; every console house read goes through `src/lib/server/house-console-read.ts` and fails closed) > **this file** > `plans/house-bots/C5-D20-REPLAN.md` > `plans/house-bots/PLAN.md` and `plans/house-bots/03-design-spec.md`.

**This is a DRAFT.** It was written outside the repository, in a scratch directory, while checkpoint C5-5b was still writing in `C:/kipindi-house-bots`. The orchestrator commits it (to `plans/house-bots/C7-SPEC.md`) after re-deriving every anchor against the post-un-build tree. Nothing here was run: no suite, no `tsc`, no build, no render, no probe.

**Provenance of every anchor.** Each anchor carries the line's TEXT so it can be re-found when the line moves. An anchor marked ✔ was re-derived by the adjudicator in this session against the working tree. Every other anchor carries text confirmed by at least one adversarial read; where the two reads disagreed about a line number, the corrected number is used and the text is the thing to trust. An anchor nobody could re-derive is marked **NOT VERIFIED** in the ruling that uses it.

**Numbering.** Commit 5 runs to 275 and checkpoint C5-5b may take 276–299. Commit 7 starts at 300. Blocks: page/S1 **300–319**, nav+RBAC **320–333**, reads+DAL **340–359**, money-as-usage **360–375**, D19-of-the-console **380–399**, visual **400–419**, and **420–431** the decisions the completeness review added (§2 Area 7). No number is reused. Gaps (334–339, 376–379) stay deliberate room for the next commit in those areas and were NOT spent by the review.

**The completeness review (2026-09-18), and what it changed.** Every suite key cited below was grepped against `package.json` (three do not exist and are created by 318; every other key exists ✔). Every anchor carrying quoted text was re-derived by machine against the working tree at commit `09850526`. Where a premise disagreed with the code the RULING was corrected in place and the correction named in its own text: **303** (the band's reads, which contradicted 347), **306** (the unset-limit count was the wrong closed list), **313** (a rotted count), **316/386** (the wrong DOM event name), **317/344/345/351** (`scripts/dal-parity.test.mts` §16 has been rewritten by C5-5b and `countFeed` now sits in its D20 NEVER list), **319** (the route-literal inventory was two of twenty-eight), **320** (the `COMMIT_7` exemption's ordering), **340** (`0.260.1` asserts `houseConsoleAudience === 0` today), **356** (a per-row wallet read nothing renders), **357** (the probe is ALREADY re-anchored), **359/412** (`SensitiveReveal` bypasses the READ-TIERS gate; the gated component is `Sensitive`), **360** (`AdminKpi` puts its own value in a `title`), **362/367/409** (`aria-valuetext` cannot take a ReactNode), **368** (the wizard's bare balance is a deviation from ruling 266 and is now Ali's Q8), **371** (its two "still calls it" claims are measured absent). Line-number drift the review measured is corrected in **431**; where a number there disagrees with a ruling's own Files block, **431 is the number to use** and the quoted TEXT is what to grep.

**Adjudication rule applied to the skeptic's verdicts.** A verdict of STANDS was applied as drafted. A verdict of AMEND was applied with the corrected text. Where a verdict read REFUTED but the skeptic supplied replacement text governing the same subject (301, 317, 364, 372, and the "REFUTED as written/as sufficient" headers on 324, 383, 394), the ruling number is KEPT and the mechanism is replaced — a refuted MECHANISM is not an abandoned DECISION, and dropping the number would leave the console unbuildable at that point. Every mechanism actually struck is listed in **§6**. Where a skeptic's refutation was itself wrong, the ruling is kept and the refutation is answered with code evidence in the ruling's own text (see 301, 306, 321, 329).

## §2 The rulings

### Area 1 — the landing page `/admin/desk` (S1), rulings 300–319

#### 300. The audience is decided in the PAGE, on the STORED role, through the console gate — and it is not decided by a grant table

**Decision.** `src/app/admin/desk/page.tsx` decides its audience before it reads anything. Its first statement resolves the session and calls `houseConsoleAudience(session?.userId, "/admin/desk")`; on false the page returns `null` and the section layout's restricted panel is the only thing painted (301). The route is made Owner-only in `roles.ts` (322) so that call takes the `isOwnerOnlyPath` branch. ⛔ No bespoke `houseConsoleOwner` export: the gate-call pin fixes the SHAPE of every gate call (declared arity, arg 0 the signed-in session's id, arg 1 a STRING LITERAL equal to this file's own console route), so a one-argument audience helper is unbuildable. ⛔ The route must NOT be left unmapped: `domainForPath` falls closed to `"ops"` and `houseConsoleAudience` would then answer `canView(role, "ops")`, which reads the DB-backed grant matrix `/admin/roles` edits live — one grant away from opening the console. Ordering is part of the ruling: the verdict is awaited FIRST, and every house read is issued only after it returns true. `/new`, `/[id]`, every tab panel, every server action and every route handler repeat it; none relies on `src/app/admin/layout.tsx` or `AdminSectionGate`.

**Why.** Ruling 259 measured it and the module says so in its own words: "a signed-in PLAYER — the holder and a trigger player too — who opened a console page as a plain document received 200 and the page's whole server payload behind the redirect, and a flight request whose router state names the admin layouts skips them altogether" ✔. Ruling 260 measured 72 of 606 non-staff responses carrying house audit rows before the gate. The landing page is not a page with a house line on it; the page IS house data end to end.

**Files.**
- `src/lib/server/house-console-read.ts:34` — `export async function houseConsoleAudience(viewerUserId: string | null | undefined, route: string): Promise<boolean> {` ✔
- `src/lib/server/house-console-read.ts:39` — `    if (isOwnerOnlyPath(route)) return isAdmin(viewer.role);` ✔
- `src/lib/server/house-console-read.ts:40` — `    return (await canView(viewer.role, domainForPath(route))) === true;` ✔
- `src/lib/server/house-console-read.ts:37` — `    const viewer = await db.user.findById(viewerUserId);` ✔ (the stored role, never the cookie)
- `src/lib/server/roles.ts:301` — `  return "ops";` (the fail-closed default that is NOT a gate)
- `scripts/lib/house-bot-reports-cases.mts:1277` — `        if (n.arguments.length !== CONSOLE_GATES[gate]) say(\`${gate} takes ${n.arguments.length} arguments, not ${CONSOLE_GATES[gate]}\`, n);` ✔
- `scripts/lib/house-bot-reports-cases.mts:1282` — `        const ownRoute = !!fileRoute && r.startsWith("/admin") && (fileRoute === r || fileRoute.startsWith(\`${r}/\`))` ✔

**Proof.** `qa:house-bot-console-probe` 4.1 — with every console route instance in `MUST_CARRY` (315), 0 hits for a player, the holder, a trigger player and a non-owner staff account, in all three request modes, with the ADMIN control non-zero. Plus `test:house-bot-console` 1.300: every `page.tsx`, `route.ts` and `actions.ts` under `src/app/admin/desk/` names a console gate before its first house read; and `houseConsoleAudience(<each non-ADMIN staff id>, "/admin/desk")` is false with an `ops` view grant WRITTEN for that role first. Mutation: delete the await from `page.tsx` and 4.1 reports the player row.

#### 301. The section gets the standard `layout.tsx` — and `AdminSectionGate` gains ONE optional `title`, because the detail route's last segment is a bot id

**Decision.** `src/app/admin/desk/layout.tsx` is the six-line section layout every other section ships (`src/app/admin/house/layout.tsx` ✔). It covers `/admin/desk`, `/new` and `/[id]`; no nested layout is added; it is given no extra ADMIN condition (that is 300's job, and a condition here would read as the gate it is not). AND `AdminSectionGate` gains one optional server-passed prop, `title?: string`, defaulting to today's `crumbsFromPath(path).at(-1)` so no other section changes, and this layout passes the neutral section word. **Reason, measured:** the gate titles its own restricted panel from the last URL segment, and `looksLikeId` returns TRUE for `hb_` + 24 hex (`/^(usr|mkt|udr|txn|kyc)_/i` misses it, but it has a digit and an underscore), so `crumbsFromPath` keeps the segment VERBATIM and the panel's heading on `/admin/desk/hb_…` would be the raw house id — an `ids`-family needle in a body the section layout streams. With the rename (320) the WORD problem disappears by construction ("desk" title-cases to "Desk"), but the ID problem does not, and it is what this prop closes.

**Why.** ⛔ The skeptic's refutation of the draft's justification is CORRECT and is recorded: `test:admin-section-gate` does NOT require a `layout.tsx` — `scripts/admin-section-gate.test.mjs:39` is `  const gated = (file) => /AdminSectionGate/.test(readFileSync(file, "utf8"));` and `:42` `    if (gated(page)) continue;`, so a page that renders the gate itself satisfies §0 (the documented `/admin`, `/admin/players` precedent). The layout is kept anyway because it is what paints `AdminRestricted` for a non-ADMIN STAFF viewer on a normal navigation and what supplies `AdminActProvider`, so 300's `null` does not leave a blank screen. The `title` prop is a §K5 one-prop kit extension, exactly like 409's `caption`.

**Files.**
- `src/app/admin/house/layout.tsx:5` — `  return <AdminSectionGate>{children}</AdminSectionGate>;` ✔
- `src/components/admin/admin-section-gate.tsx:30` — `export async function AdminSectionGate({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {` ✔ (no title prop today)
- `src/components/admin/admin-section-gate.tsx:33` — `  const title = crumbsFromPath(path || "/admin").at(-1) ?? "Restricted";` ✔
- `src/components/admin/admin-section-gate.tsx:36-37` — `  if (!session || !user || !isStaffRole(user.role) || !path.startsWith("/admin")) {` / `    return <AdminRestricted title={title} need="a staff sign-in" />;` ✔
- `src/components/admin/admin-nav-groups.ts:45` — `    CRUMB_LABELS[p] ?? (looksLikeId(p) ? p : p.replace(/-/g, " ").replace(/\b\w/g, (c) => c.toUpperCase())))];` ✔
- `src/components/admin/admin-nav-groups.ts:56-58` — `  if (/^(usr|mkt|udr|txn|kyc)_/i.test(p)) return true;` / `  if (!/\d/.test(p)) return false;` / `  return p.includes("_") || /^[a-z0-9]{20,}$/i.test(p);` ✔

**Proof.** `test:house-bot-console` 1.301: the gate rendered for a PLAYER, a SUPPORT officer and a signed-out requester on each of the three paths INCLUDING `/admin/desk/hb_<24 hex>` — `houseHits` over the rendered markup empty in all nine, with a planted control (drop the prop and the case names the id). Plus `test:admin-section-gate` §0 green with the three pages gated, and every existing section's panel byte-identical (the default branch).

#### 302. `force-dynamic`; an unknown `?tab=` falls back to the roster; `notFound()` is reserved for a missing RECORD

**Decision.** `export const dynamic = "force-dynamic"` on all three route files. `?tab=` resolves against the closed list (319); ANY other value — absent, empty, misspelled, or an array from a repeated parameter — renders the `roster` panel with exactly one `aria-current="page"`, and never `notFound()` and never a redirect (a query string is not a resource, and a redirect on every bare visit rewrites a bookmarked URL). `notFound()` is called in exactly one place in the section: `/admin/desk/[id]` when `houseBotStore.get(id)` returns null AFTER 300's verdict has passed — and it is not an oracle, because a viewer outside the audience receives the SAME answer for a real id and an invented one (399). A REMOVED bot is NOT a 404: it renders read-only, and because `listNonRemoved` excludes it in both twins the roster carries no row for it, so its only doors are the history tab's row link and a typed id (358).

**Why.** Every figure is live money against a live cap, so nothing may be cached (and see 356: a cached render is a payload computed for one audience and served to another). DA §K 7f requires the active panel to be chosen server-side from `?tab=`, so an unrecognised value must resolve to a real panel or the page renders nothing.

**Files.**
- `src/app/admin/house/page.tsx:375` — `        {tab === "position" && (<>` (the shipped tab idiom; also `:543`, `:624`)
- `src/lib/server/house-bot-dal.ts:1318` — `  get(id: string, tx?: HouseTx): Promise<StoredHouseBot | null>;`
- `src/lib/server/house-bot-dal.ts:2036` — memory twin: `status !== "REMOVED"`; `:3176` — Prisma: `WHERE "status" <> 'REMOVED'`
- `docs/DESIGN_AUTHORITY.md:2108` — `   **Inactive panels are NOT RENDERED** — the active panel is chosen server-side from \`?tab=\`.`

**Proof.** `test:house-bot-console` 1.302: (a) `?tab=` absent / empty / `limitz` / repeated each render the roster with exactly one `aria-current="page"`; (b) an invented id throws not-found for an ADMIN and returns the identical status and body for a player as a REAL id does; (c) a REMOVED bot renders read-only, is absent from the roster, and is reached from the history tab.

#### 303. The KPI band is four tiles of cap usage and one count pair — no results tile, ever

**Decision.** `KpiGrid cols="4"` carries exactly four `AdminKpi`, above the rail, in this order: **Stake today** (used, against `gCapDailyStakeTzs`), **Loss today** (`max(0, projectedLossToday)`, against `gCapDailyLossTzs`, on the PROJECTED basis because that is the basis the cap is checked against, clamped because a cohort in profit uses none of the loss cap and a negative "used" figure is a result), **Open exposure** (against `gCapOpenExposureTzs`), **Bots** (`countLive()` of `maxDesignatedBots`, value "2 of 5", delta "designated of max"). Their COMPOSITION is 404's: one compact amount in `value`, and the limit named in `delta` as a proportion with NO second amount. ⛔ Never `gold`, `tone`, `pulse` or `series`; `deltaDir` stays the default `flat`. ⛔ **CORRECTED by the completeness review — the draft named the two reads 347 FORBIDS.** The three money values are the JS SUM of the SAME per-bot rows the roster renders: ONE `houseDayBooks(dayKey)` (`book.ts:101` ✔, one query, every bot) and ONE `houseBookStore.openExposure(null)` (`house-bot-dal.ts:1582` ✔, per-bot rows), inside the single gated read (347). ⛔ NOT `houseDayBook(dayKey, null)` and NOT `houseOpenExposure(null)`: those are a second population, and a stake settling between them and the rows makes the band disagree with the column beneath it while each figure is individually correct. 347 governs; this ruling owns only the tile SET and its wording. ⛔ STRUCK and not replaced: a "Today's net" tile, a bare "Live exposure" tile with no cap beside it, a bets-placed/skipped counter with no configured denominator, and any lifetime figure. Bets-per-day usage lives on the limits tab; the activity tab's badge carries pending intents.

**Why.** Ruling 266; D20's default for Commit 7 ("no results/P&L report, CSV or per-market house line beyond what a control needs"). DA §K 7d requires the band above the rail to answer "is something wrong right now" — four readings of how close the feature is to each stop that would silence it answer exactly that, where a net answers "how did we do", which is the question D20 removed. Measured: `countLive` and `listNonRemoved` filter the IDENTICAL population in both twins, so the Bots tile and the roster's row count are one fact read once.

**Files.**
- `src/components/admin/admin-body.tsx:65` — `  "4":        "grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-4",                // the console default — 33 bands`
- `src/components/admin/admin-shell.tsx:367` — `export function AdminKpi({` ✔; `:378` — `  deltaDir = "flat",` ✔; `:394` — `  tone?: "danger" | "success";` ✔ (there is no amber, so the words must carry a block — 304)
- `src/lib/server/house-bot/book.ts:101` ✔ — `export async function houseDayBooks(dayKey: string, tx?: HouseTx): Promise<Map<string, HouseDayBook>> {` (the band's and the roster's ONE day read; `houseDayBook` at `:110` ✔ is the per-bot form the DETAIL page uses, not the band's)
- `src/lib/server/house-bot-dal.ts:1582` ✔ — `  openExposure(houseBotId: string | null, tx?: HouseTx): Promise<Array<{ houseBotId: string; openStakeTzs: number }>>;` (the per-bot rows the band sums)
- `src/lib/server/house-bot/cap-precheck.ts:66-68` ✔ — the three global money refusals (`GLOBAL_DAILY_STAKE`, `GLOBAL_LOSS_PROJECTED`, `GLOBAL_EXPOSURE`)
- `prisma/schema.prisma:1962` — `  maxDesignatedBots                Int       @default(5)`
- `src/lib/server/house-bot-dal.ts:2039-2041` / `:3179-3182` — `countLive`'s two twins, both `status <> 'REMOVED'`

**Proof.** `test:house-bot-console` 1.303: against a fixture with stake 120,000 / projected loss −8,000 / exposure 64,000 / 2 bots and caps 500,000 / 100,000 / 300,000 / 5, the band renders four tiles, each with exactly ONE amount and a delta naming its limit; the loss tile reads "TZS 0"; the band's text contains no "net", no "lifetime" and no amount inside a `delta`; and the Bots tile equals the roster's row count on every fixture.

#### 304. An unset cap renders as a BLOCK, never a zero; a failed read renders `unavailable`

**Decision.** When a cap column is NULL the tile's value is "Not set" and its delta is "no bot can bet — set it on Limits →"; the used figure is NOT shown in that state (a used amount with no limit is a bare money figure, which 266 forbids). The tile is not tinted — `AdminKpi` has no amber tone ✔ and colour is never the only signal — so the words carry it, reinforced by the strip's ON-disabled sentence (306) and the limits tab's count badge (312). When a read THROWS the tile is rendered with `unavailable` — the component's own "n/a · couldn't compute" state, whose title already says "It is NOT zero" ✔ — never a fabricated TZS 0 and never an empty tile. ⛔ One tile's failure never suppresses the other three and never suppresses the roster.

**Why.** Measured: `over(cap, value)` is `cap == null || value > cap`, so an unset cap REFUSES every stake — an unset global cap is not "no limit", it is a total stop. "TZS 0 of TZS 0" would tell an officer the opposite of the truth.

**Files.**
- `src/lib/server/house-bot/cap-precheck.ts:43` — `const over = (cap: number | null, value: number): boolean => cap == null || value > cap;`
- `src/components/admin/admin-shell.tsx:408` — `  if (unavailable) {` ✔; `:412` — `        title="This figure could not be computed — a data read failed. It is NOT zero."` ✔
- `prisma/schema.prisma:1953` — `  gCapDailyStakeTzs                BigInt?` (nullable: the state is reachable by construction)

#### 306. The master-switch strip: chip, sentence, `Toggle tone="brand"`, and an ON-disabled reason whose COUNT is derived

**Decision.** One `AdminCard padding="p-4"` at the top of `AdminBody`, above the KPI band, on every tab. Left: `Chip size="sm"` reading "On" (`TONE_CHIP.green`) or "Off" (`TONE_CHIP.slate`) plus a `text-body-sm` sentence — OFF: "House bots are off. No bot will place a bet." for every off state, whatever the cause; ON: "On since HH:MM:SS EAT · switched by <name> · reason: <reason>", formatted on the server. Right: the visible label "Master switch" beside `Toggle tone="brand"` with `aria-label="House bots master switch"`. ⛔ Never `tone="gold"` (gold is earned money only) and ⛔ never `tone="claret"` (claret means ON MEANS STOPPED; here ON means money can move). When the switch cannot be turned on because global limits are unset, the text beside the Toggle is "Set N global limits first →", a Link to `?tab=limits#limits-first-unset`, where N is COUNTED at render time over **`REQUIRED_FOR_MASTER_ON`** (`src/lib/house-bot/rules.ts:857-866` ✔) — never a typed number and never a hand-copied list. ⛔ **CORRECTED by the completeness review.** The draft counted "the globals the seam refuses a plain stake on" and named SIX (`gCapPerMarketTzs`, `gCapDailyStakeTzs`, `gCapDailyLossTzs`, `gCapOpenExposureTzs`, `gMaxBetsPerMinute`, `gMaxBetsPerDay`). That is the wrong closed list for this sentence: the sentence explains why the switch cannot be turned ON, and what blocks the switch is `REQUIRED_FOR_MASTER_ON`, measured **EIGHT** members — those six PLUS `gCounterPerPlayerPerDay` and `gCounterPerPlayerTzsPerDay`. With only the two counterparty limits unset the draft's count renders 0, the strip says nothing, and the ON press fails with no explanation on screen — the silent-refusal defect this strip exists to prevent. The count is `REQUIRED_FOR_MASTER_ON.filter((f) => control[f] == null).length`, folded in JS from the control row already read (344), and 364's caption for the same fields says the same thing on the form. The ON press opens the Master-ON modal (415); the OFF press is the kill switch with no dialog, and its overlay states the STATE and ⛔ no held amount (370).

**Why.** DA §K 7d: a control that starts or stops something in production stays above the rail on every tab. The count must come from the data because a typed "3" is a copy of a fact whose home is `REQUIRED_FOR_MASTER_ON` — and it is already wrong: that list holds EIGHT fields (measured, `rules.ts:858-865` ✔), and the seam refuses a plain stake on six of them, a counter stake on two more. ⛔ RECORDED, one deviation and not two: `PLAN.md:682` (X5) is CORRECT — there is no `<Toggle tone="gold">` CALL SITE (the only `tone="gold"` in `src/` is a `<Stat>` at `src/app/admin/config/fee-simulator.tsx:143`, and `toggle.tsx:8` records that the gold boolean it replaced had zero call sites), so X5 is a prohibition on an API the code does not use and PLAN needs no mark. The real deviation is `03:126`'s "Set 3".

- `src/lib/status-tone.ts:78` — `export const TONE_CHIP = {`

Plus `test:tab-anchors`, whose population is every source link carrying `/admin/<route>…#anchor`, for `?tab=limits#limits-first-unset`.

#### 307. Every client file of this route is house-free

**Decision.** In every `"use client"` file of this section and its whole import graph: (a) no word, identifier or bounded id of `scripts/lib/house-bot-vocabulary.mjs` appears after type-and-comment stripping — not in a string, a prop name, a component name or a variable (so `houseBotsOn` is forbidden: the words family is matched case-insensitively ✔ and hits "houseBots"); (b) no href literal — every link target, including the rail's `href`s, the limits anchor and the row links, arrives as a PROP from the server (330); (c) no value import of anything under `src/lib/house-bot/`, `src/lib/server/house-bot/`, `src/lib/server/house-bot-dal.ts` or `src/lib/server/house-console-read.ts`, and no `import type` from them either (384); (d) every imported server-action binding has a neutral export name (382). All house copy — the strip sentences, the KPI labels and deltas, the column headers, the tab labels, the Callout titles — is written in SERVER modules and reaches the browser only as a per-request RSC prop on this ADMIN-gated route.

**Why.** Ruling 174, and this is its first console instance. The two enforcing guards are real and complete: `test:house-bot-disclosure` §1 walks every `"use client"` module in `src/` and its whole value-import graph, and `verify:house-bot-bundle` reads every file of `.next/static`, the prerendered documents and `public/`, content AND path. Measured: a client chunk of this codebase carries `createServerReference)("…", u.callServer, void 0, u.findSourceMapURL, "updateGlobalConfigAction")` and another carries the `ROUTE_KEYS`/`CRUMB_LABELS` tables verbatim, so an imported action name and any literal in a client module ship as written.

Every branch carries `meta` with the server-formatted switch-off time and a body naming the way forward. ⛔ A stale `offCause` left on the control row while `enabled === true` renders NO Callout. ⛔ RECORDED DEVIATION: `03:127` names only two causes and would render nothing at all for SUNSET — the state in which the whole feature has been withdrawn.

#### 309. Engine health reads the DURABLE beats THROUGH the gate, and an unreadable health state never renders as healthy

**Decision.** The S5 Callout's facts come from ONE gated read, `houseEngineForConsole(viewerUserId, "/admin/desk")`, which inside the gate module reads (a) `houseEngineHealthFor` for the schema state and this replica's in-process facts and (b) `houseBotRuntimeStore.listInstances()` for the DURABLE beats — `beat:planner` and every `beat:poller:<INSTANCE_ID>` row, written on the DATABASE clock. ⛔ The in-process `houseBotEngineHealth()` figures are NOT the staleness source: they describe the process rendering the page, which is not necessarily the process running the engine, so a healthy engine on another replica would render as dead. Those figures may be shown only labelled as THIS server's. A failed beat read, `{ readable: false }`, or a schema state that is not ready renders the SAME Callout with `meta` "Last seen: unknown" (354). The Callout is stably keyed so a 20 s refresh does not re-announce it. ⛔ ONE AUDIENCE, PINNED: `houseEngineHealthFor` decides on `inHouseAlertAudience` (`role === "ADMIN"`), and a case asserts it answers identically to `houseConsoleAudience` on this route for every role — two audience functions on one page is the drift this area exists to stop.

#### 310. The roster's eight columns — every one a control fact, money second and third, as usage

**Decision.** `AdminCard padding="p-0"` › `ScrollX label="Bot roster"` › `table.admin-tbl`, rows from the gated roster read (oldest designation first), with exactly these columns: (1) **Bot** — the label, and `Player #XXXXXX` beneath it in `text-caption` (346); (2) **Loss today (projected)** — `td.tabular`, that bot's `projectedLossTzs` clamped at 0 against `capDailyLossTzs`, in 361's grammar, or "Not set" (the basis is in the header: 373(b), which the review corrected to agree with this line); (3) **Exposure** — `td.tabular`, that bot's open stake against `capOpenExposureTzs`, or "Not set"; (4) **Status** — the chip, with the AUTO_PAUSED reason and its way out on a second `text-caption` line that wraps (311); (5) **Bets today** — "14 of 200" against `freqMaxPerDay`, or "Not set"; (6) **Last bet** — relative text with the absolute EAT time in `title`, "—" when never; (7) **Products** — the saved scope words; (8) `.row-link` "open →". ⛔ STRUCK: the "Today net" column (266) and the "Live balance" column — a balance is headroom, not usage, and expressing it as "used X of Y" would not be honest; the fact is not lost (the activity feed's `CAP_BALANCE_FLOOR` sentence, and the floor STATE on the bot page, 368). ⛔ No derived "Can bet now" column: it cannot be computed without a market id, so it would be a partial duplicate of the seam's own order of checks. **Empty-state precedence, decided:** switch OFF wins over "no bots yet" (the switch is the state, the roster is the detail), and a FAILED read wins over both and renders `AdminLoadError what="the roster"` (355).

#### 312. Four tabs — roster (default), activity, limits, history — as a URL-backed line rail, counts through `CountBadge`

**Decision.** `Tabs variant="line"` with four `TabItem`s each carrying `href` of `?tab=<key>` from 319's one home: `roster` (the DEFAULT), `activity`, `limits`, `history`. The active panel is chosen server-side and inactive panels are NOT rendered. Counts: `activity` carries pending intents, `limits` carries the unset-global count (the same derived number as 306, from one read), both through `TabItem.count` → the kit's `CountBadge`; `roster` and `history` carry none. ⛔ Because `CountBadge` renders nothing at zero, a count may never stand in for a read's health: if a count read FAILS the tab shows no badge AND the panel renders `AdminLoadError` for that subject (355). The rail's `data-section-rail` is stamped by the KIT, not by this page — measured on the LINE variant at `tabs.tsx:572-579` ✔ (`isNav ? <nav … data-section-rail="" …>`), NOT at `:434`, which is the PILL variant's nav and is the anchor the draft cited; what the page must not do is stamp `data-filter-rail` on it (that key belongs to the activity tab's filter row alone, 410). ⛔ **A TAB EXISTS ONLY WITH ITS PANEL (added by the completeness review, and it changes the build order).** The closed list in 319 starts at `["roster"]` and GROWS with the commit's steps — `limits` at step 3, `activity` and `history` at step 5 — because a rail option whose panel is not written is a dead control, and §3's steps build the four panels across three checkpoints. The rail therefore renders exactly the keys the closed list holds at that step, 302's fallback resolves every other value to `roster`, and 1.312's four-key case is written at step 5 with the fourth panel, not at step 1 (§4 corrected). A tab is never shipped rendering "not built yet": no pending states. The four tabs qualify under DA §K 7a on all three tests, stated for the record: the panels are independent sections, none of them the page's length (the roster is bounded by `maxDesignatedBots`, 1–20; activity and history are paginated at 20); they are alternative TASKS read one at a time; and nothing load-bearing sits behind a click, because the strip, both Callouts and the four tiles are above the rail on every tab (306, 406). ⛔ `scripts/design-gate/routes.mjs` gains the page BARE, with no `?tab=` entry (329).

**Decision.** `AdminPageHead actions` is `<Button size="md" variant="primary" href="/admin/desk/new">Designate an account</Button>`, and when the roster is full the button is rendered disabled with a VISIBLE reason beside it (never a `title` only) whose text is `DESIGNATE_COPY.rosterFull(count, max)` — "The roster is full (5 of 5). Remove a bot or raise Max designated bots on Limits →" — as a Link to `?tab=limits`. The count and max are the CONFIGURED values (`maxDesignatedBots`, range 1–20, default 5), never the literal 5. ⛔ The words are not re-typed and not shortened to "Roster full (5 of 5)": the server owns that sentence and the wizard's own refusal uses it. When the roster-count read FAILS the button is rendered ENABLED with no reason — the wizard's `ROSTER_FULL` refusal is the backstop, and a failed count must not silently forbid a legitimate designation. ⛔ `hidden` never goes on a `.btn`.

**Files.**
- `src/lib/server/house-bot/designation.ts:222` — `  rosterFull: (n: number, max: number) => \`The roster is full (${n} of ${max}). Remove a bot or raise Max designated bots on Limits →\`,`
- `src/lib/server/house-bot/eligibility.ts:301-302` — the short form and the full sentence with the `?tab=limits` href (the two-spellings problem this closes)
- `src/lib/house-bot/rules.ts:778` — `  maxDesignatedBots: num("limits", "Roster and alerts", "Max designated bots", "count", 1, 20, {`

**Proof.** `test:house-bot-console` 1.314: (a) with 5 of 5, the rendered head text equals `DESIGNATE_COPY.rosterFull(5, 5)` character for character and links to `?tab=limits`; (b) with a rejecting count read, the button is enabled and no reason shows; (c) no file under the section contains "roster is full" or "Roster full".

#### 315. The page must be MEASURED by the probe, not merely enumerated

**Decision.** Two things land with the page. (a) The tab selection is written in the shipped idiom — `{tab === "roster" && (…)}` and so on — because the probe discovers a page's tabs by matching `/tab === "([a-z-]+)"/g` in the page FILE, and any other shape (a `TAB_KEYS.includes` switch, a lookup map) leaves three of four panels silently unrequested; the checkpoint MUST read the printed `2.routes` line and confirm 5 route instances for `/admin/desk`, not 1. (b) `"/admin/desk"` joins the probe's `MUST_CARRY`, so a console page the ADMIN control reads as SILENT is a FAILURE rather than a `4.nm` NOT MEASURED line, and the house fixture leaves at least one designated bot so the roster is non-empty. Recorded so it is not assumed: the other four `?tab=` instances stay out of `silent` because 306's strip sits above the rail on EVERY tab and its sentence "House bots are off. No bot will place a bet." is itself a words-family hit under flags "gi" ✔ — if a later ruling removes the strip from any tab, that tab joins `MUST_CARRY` in the same change.

**Files.**
- `scripts/house-bot-console-probe.mts:286` — `    const tabs = [...new Set([...code.matchAll(/tab === "([a-z-]+)"/g)].map((m) => m[1]))];`
- `scripts/house-bot-console-probe.mts:309` — `  ok("2.routes · every console page is enumerated from disk and requested", pageRoutes.length >= 50 && ROUTES.length >= pageRoutes.length, …)`
- `scripts/house-bot-console-probe.mts:409` — `  const MUST_CARRY = ["/admin/audit", …];`
- `scripts/house-bot-console-probe.mts:413` — `  for (const r of silent) notMeasured(\`4.nm · ${r}\`, "the ADMIN control carries no house data there, so its absence for the other viewers proves nothing about that page");`

**Proof.** The probe itself: 4.3 fails if `/admin/desk` is in `MUST_CARRY` and the ADMIN's response carries no house data, and `2.routes`' printed count is the record that all four tabs were requested. Add `/admin/desk` to `MUST_CARRY` with a non-matching tab shape and 4.3 reports it.

#### 316. One `RefreshPoller` in the strip, on `LIVE_ROUND_MS`, enabled from REACT state

**Decision.** Exactly one `RefreshPoller` on the page, inside the strip (which is above the rail on every tab, so no tab switch remounts it), `intervalMs={LIVE_ROUND_MS}` (20 s), `enabled = (switchOn || anyBotActiveOrAutoPaused) && !formDirty && !anyDialogOpen`. ⛔ `anyDialogOpen` is READ FROM REACT STATE — the flag each dialog's own open/closed state sets — and NEVER from a DOM query for `dialog`/`[role=dialog]`: a CLOSED dialog left in the DOM once silenced the reality check and the social links on `/markets`, and the correct question is always "is it VISIBLE". ⛔ **CORRECTED by the completeness review — the draft named an event that is never dispatched on `window`.** `notification:new` is the SSE WIRE type (`src/app/api/events/route.ts:37`, `:39`); the window CustomEvent `useEventStream` dispatches for it is **`50pick:sse:notification`** (`src/lib/use-event-stream.ts:22-27` ✔, `EVENT_MAP`), and `RefreshPoller`'s own immediate-refresh listener is its `eventName` prop, whose DEFAULT is `50pick:refresh` (`refresh-poller.tsx:21` ✔) and which no admin surface dispatches. So the wiring is exactly: the strip mounts `useEventStream()` itself (nothing else does on `/admin` — `AppShell` returns bare children, `app-shell.tsx:70-72` ✔) and passes `eventName="50pick:sse:notification"` to the one `RefreshPoller`; no kind is compared (386). A strip that mounts the poller with its default event name is live only on the 20 s interval — which is not a failure, and is why 353's threshold is stated against `LIVE_ROUND_MS` and not against the push. When `enabled` is false the poller registers NOTHING — the component's own contract: `:29` "⭐ E-102 · false ⇒ register NOTHING — no interval, no listener." with `:44` `    if (!enabled) return;` INSIDE the effect. The 20 s cadence and 353's 30 s staleness threshold are ordered so a stale engine is visible within at most two polls. RECORDED, not fixed here: E-381's remaining half (a session revoked mid-visit can leave a polling page blank) applies to this page like every other and is not a Commit 7 debt.

**Files.**
- `src/components/ui/refresh-poller.tsx:19` — `export function RefreshPoller({`; `:29`, `:34`, `:44` — the disabled contract and its implementation
- `src/lib/refresh-cadence.ts:46` — `export const LIVE_ROUND_MS = 20_000;`

**Proof.** `test:house-bot-console` 1.316: (a) exactly one `RefreshPoller` in the rendered tree on each of the four tabs; (b) `enabled` false with the switch off and no live bot, true with the switch on, false while a dialog flag is set; (c) no file under the section calls `querySelector` with `dialog` or `role="dialog"`. Plus `qa:chaos` and `qa:pending-bar` in the client pass.

#### 317. The history tab is paged by NUMBER, which needs one new counting reader and one new page reader — in both twins

**Decision.** The landing page's `history` tab shows control events AND every bot's events together, newest first, 20 per page through `AdminPagination`, with columns When · Event (a lexicon word) · Change (from → to) · Reason (wraps) · By ("System — house bot engine" when the actor is null); zero rows render `AdminTableEmpty` ("No changes yet."), a failed read `AdminLoadError`. ⛔ It may NOT be driven by a keyset page: `Pagination` requires `total: number` ✔ and draws a numbered `?page=N` link for every page in its window ✔, while `Page<T>` is `{ rows, nextCursor }` with no total ✔ — a numbered pager over a cursor reader links to pages nothing can serve. So Commit 7 adds TWO members to `HouseBotEventStore`, in BOTH twins with `test:dal-parity` cases (ruling 177): `listAll(opts: { limit: number; offset?: number; kinds?: readonly HouseBotEventKind[] })` and `countAll(opts: { kinds?: readonly HouseBotEventKind[] })`, each deriving its population from ONE shared named predicate per twin (the `feedWhere`/`memFeedMatches` shape of 345). ⛔ Not built as a capped `listByKinds` call with a "showing the latest 200" note: a cap presented as a page is a list that silently ends, and these events are the only durable record of who switched what. ⛔ And ONE paging idiom on this page: the activity feed pages the same way (344, 345), because two idioms on one page is the drift §0a forbids.

- `src/lib/server/house-bot-dal.ts:72` — `export type Page<T> = { rows: T[]; nextCursor: KeysetCursor | null };` ✔
- `src/lib/server/house-bot-dal.ts:1408` — `export interface HouseBotEventStore {`; `:1415` — `listByBot(…)` (the keyset shape it is NOT copying); `:1417` — `listByKinds(…)` (a bare `limit`, no cursor, no total)
- `src/lib/server/house-bot-dal.ts:1779-1781` — `function pageLimit(limit: number): number {` … `  return Math.max(1, Math.min(500, Math.floor(limit)));` (why a total assembled by paging would be a lie: the clamp binds the PAGE, never the count)

### Area 2 — nav, routes, RBAC and the client chunk, rulings 320–333

#### 320. The route segment carries no house word and no house id: the console ships at `/admin/desk`, with `[id]` as its dynamic segment

**Decision.** Commit 7 does NOT create `src/app/admin/house-bots/`. The section is `src/app/admin/desk/` — `/admin/desk`, `/admin/desk/new`, `/admin/desk/[id]` — nav key `desk`, label "Desk". The segment `house-bots` is never used in a URL, a directory name, a route key, a route-domain prefix, an owner-only prefix, a nav href, a notification href, a design-gate route list or a drive list. **The dynamic segment is `[id]`, not `[botId]`:** `scripts/lib/house-bot-comms-cases.mts:260` resolves every notifier href against `src/app` allowing exactly `["[id]", "[positionId]", "[marketId]", "[slug]"]` ✔ at each level, so `[botId]` would report the console's own hrefs as unresolvable, and widening that resolver to admit a new segment name buys nothing the platform's own spelling does not. **In the same pass, and the ORDER matters (corrected by the completeness review):** `scripts/lib/house-bot-comms-cases.mts:249`'s `const COMMIT_7 = ["/admin/house-bots"];` ✔ becomes `["/admin/desk"]` **in the same commit that re-points all 28 route literals (319)** — not later. The reason is mechanical: `7.2` resolves every notifier href that a house alert actually produces against `src/app`, skipping only paths under `COMMIT_7`; so a `COMMIT_7` of `["/admin/desk"]` beside hrefs still reading `/admin/house-bots/<id>` fails 7.2 on day one, and a `COMMIT_7` holding BOTH prefixes fails `7.3` (`COMMIT_7.length === 1` ✔). Re-pointing the literals and swapping the one constant together keeps the list at one entry and both cases green from step 1, while `/admin/desk/[id]` and `/admin/desk/new` are still unbuilt (that is exactly what the exemption is for). Once all three pages exist BOTH that constant and case `7.3` ✔ are DELETED together — 7.3 exists only to stop the exemption list growing, so when the list is empty its subject is gone, and what replaces it is case 7.2 resolving the console's hrefs for real instead of skipping them. `desk` is chosen because it collides with nothing: measured ✔, `grep -rn "admin/desk|\"desk\"" src/ scripts/` returns nothing, no `/admin/de*` prefix exists in `ROUTE_KEYS` or `ROUTE_DOMAINS`, no NAV_GROUPS key `desk` exists, and it matches none of the three vocabulary families. Ali may rename the word (§7 Q1); he may not restore a house word in the segment.

**Why.** A route prefix cannot stay server-side: the RBAC gate needs it in `src/lib/server/roles.ts` and the sidebar highlight needs it in `src/components/admin/admin-nav-groups.ts`, and BOTH are in the client import graph — measured, `admin-nav-groups.ts` is value-imported by three `"use client"` modules and `roles.ts` by the client `roles-matrix.tsx`. And the graph is not theory: a built client chunk of this codebase carries `let t={updown:"Up & Down",aml:"AML",…}` and `[["/admin/live","live"],["/admin/finance","finance"],["/admin/house","house"],["/admin/staff","staff"],["/admin/roles","roles"]` verbatim — that is `CRUMB_LABELS` and `ROUTE_KEYS` shipped to the browser. `house[_ -]?bots?` is a needle ✔ and `/admin/house` is exempt as a benign look-alike ✔ while `/admin/house-bots` is not. Two further consequences avoided: `admin/layout.tsx` answers a non-staff request with `redirect("/auth/admin?next=<the path>")`, so the feature's name would be built into a response to a player; and the probe reads response bodies with the shared `houseHits`, so such an echo could be cleared only by narrowing the needles — which this ruling refuses. C5-SPEC ruling 255's Commit-5 `.next/static` conditional applied to the per-bot CSV route D20 struck and is moot; its Commit 7 clause ("a route segment name checked by 175's path scan") is satisfied by renaming now.

- `src/components/admin/admin-crumbs.tsx:26` / `admin-sidebar-nav.tsx:5` / `admin-mobile-nav.tsx:7` — the three client importers (each file's line 1 is `"use client";`)
- `src/app/admin/roles/roles-matrix.tsx:18` — `import { ADMIN_DOMAINS, DOMAIN_LABEL, DOMAIN_SUMMARY, ROLE_LABEL, type AdminDomain, type Role } from "@/lib/server/roles";` (a VALUE import; line 1 is `"use client";`)
- `scripts/lib/house-bot-vocabulary.mjs:34` ✔ and `:92` ✔ — the words, and the benign list that exempts bare `/admin/house`
- `scripts/lib/house-bot-comms-cases.mts:249`, `:253`, `:260`, `:268` ✔ — the exemption, the skip, the dynamic allowlist, case 7.3

#### 321. `admin-nav-groups.ts` gains exactly one neutral owner-only item and one `ROUTE_KEYS` pair — and no `CRUMB_LABELS` entry

**Decision.** Add to `NAV_GROUPS`, in the Money group immediately after the `/admin/house` item: `{ href: "/admin/desk", label: "Desk", key: "desk", domain: "ops", ownerOnly: true }`. Add to `ROUTE_KEYS` exactly `["/admin/desk", "desk"]`; its position is free ✔. Add NOTHING to `CRUMB_LABELS`: the title-caser already yields "Desk" ✔, and a `CRUMB_LABELS` entry is the one place a neutral segment could be mapped BACK to the house word inside a client chunk. **The label carries no Swahili gloss, because `NavItem` has no `sw` field** — measured ✔: `NavItem` is `{ href; label; key; badge?; domain; ownerOnly?; allStaff? }` and only `NavGroup.group` is `{ en; sw }`; a gloss would be a kit change nobody asked for, and the section's gloss question is settled by 403. `PLAN.md:487`'s "`ROUTE_KEYS` entry **before** `/admin/house`" is moot and the reason is recorded so it is never reintroduced: `activeKeyFromPath` matches with a BARE `path.startsWith(prefix)`, so `/admin/house-bots` would have resolved to key "house" and highlighted the owner's book, while `domainForPath` matches `path === prefix || path.startsWith(prefix + "/")` and would NOT have collided — the two resolvers disagree by design, and a future sibling prefix is checked against the bare form. **NO COMMENT IN THIS FILE NAMES THE FEATURE, and that is a policy with NO static proof:** `test:house-bot-disclosure` 1.1 strips comments before scanning and minification removes them from the bundle, so neither absence guard can fail on a comment here; it is held by review and by the repository being PUBLIC (W20). Do not cite a suite for it. In the same pass correct `:37` to say THREE client components import this module.

**Why.** `ownerOnly: true` is the only flag that removes the item before serialisation: `filterNavGroups` returns `!!it.allStaff || (it.ownerOnly ? isOwner : set.has(it.domain))`, it is called SERVER-side in `AdminSidebar` and `AdminTopBar`, and `isOwner` comes from the STORED role ✔ (`admin/layout.tsx:167-169` reads `u!.role`). So the item never enters a non-owner's payload, and because the item is itself neutral it is safe in a file that ships to the browser. `assertNavKeysResolve` requires every `ROUTE_KEYS` key to be OWNED by a nav item, which is why the pair and the item land together.

ROUTE_DOMAINS ORDER IS IRRELEVANT here — the matcher can never let `/admin/house` shadow `/admin/desk` — so no ordering claim is made. `OWNER_ONLY_PREFIXES`, not the `ops` domain, is the gate: no non-ADMIN role holds `ops` in `DEFAULT_GRANTS`, but the Owner can grant `ops` view to any role live at `/admin/roles` with no redeploy, so an `ops`-only argument is one grant edit away from opening the console. #### 323. If Ali releases the word, it comes from a server-only module and is applied AFTER `filterNavGroups` — never from `admin-nav-groups.ts`

**Decision.** The built default is 321's neutral label. If Ali answers (2) to §7 Q1, the word is added by exactly this mechanism and no other: a new server-only module `src/lib/server/house-bot/console-nav.ts` exports the display word and nothing else; `AdminSidebar` and `AdminTopBar` substitute it onto the `key === "desk"` item AFTER `filterNavGroups(viewDomains, isOwner)` returns and ONLY when `isOwner` is true; `admin-nav-groups.ts` keeps the neutral label as the value that ships, and the client components keep rendering `it.label` with no knowledge of the substitution. EVERY head title in the section takes its text as a PROP from a server component reading that same module — no page, loader or client file under `src/app/admin/desk/**` contains a title literal — so `03:117`'s `title="House bots"` and `03:243`'s `title={label}` are values, not literals, and one answer settles them in one place. The substitution is never hoisted above the filter, never memoised across viewers, and never moved into `admin-nav-groups.ts`.

**Why.** `src/components/admin/admin-shell.tsx` is MEASURED server-only: no `"use client"` module imports it (the client files that mention it do so in comments, which the walker strips; `src/app/admin/retention/purge-chain-card.tsx:6` states the rule in as many words). So a word placed there reaches a browser only as a per-request RSC prop on an admin route, for a viewer the stored-role check has already admitted — which is what ruling 174 permits and what C5-SPEC ruling 255 already directed. Putting the module under `src/lib/server/house-bot/` makes the placement self-guarding: `scripts/house-bot-disclosure.test.mts:124` builds `houseModules` from `/[\\/]house-bot[\\/]|house-bot-dal/` over the client graph and `:125` fails BY NAME the moment a client file reaches it.

#### 324. Every page and route handler in the section decides on the STORED role before it renders or reads, and refuses with a neutral panel — never a redirect

**Decision.** The section layout renders `AdminSectionGate` (301). In ADDITION the FIRST statement of every `page.tsx` under `src/app/admin/desk/**` and of any `route.ts` added there is: read the session, call `houseConsoleAudience(session?.userId, "/admin/desk")` (a STRING LITERAL, this file's own console route or its section prefix — the gate-call pin requires it ✔), and on false return the neutral restricted panel — before any data read, any import-time house value and any JSX that could contain a house word. The refusal is a rendered panel, never `redirect()` and never `notFound()`, so the console itself builds no destination URL carrying the requested path. `src/app/admin/staff/page.tsx:32` (`if (!session || !isAdmin(session.role)) {`) is the SHAPE precedent and explicitly NOT the check: it reads the cookie's photograph of the role, where `houseConsoleAudience` reads `db.user.findById(...).role` ✔. A route handler under this section returns a body only for the audience; for anyone else it answers as a nonexistent sibling under `/admin/` answers (399).

**Why.** Ruling 259, measured, in the gate module's own words ✔. Everything the console prints is house vocabulary: its head title, its tab labels, its `feed-copy.ts` sentences, its status words. `house-console-read.ts` gates the READS; nothing today gates the page's own literal copy, and literal copy is the larger surface. The section gate stays because it is what paints the panel on a normal navigation and what supplies `AdminActProvider`; the page gate stays because the section gate can be skipped.

#### 330. No console href literal and no route key appears in any client module

**Decision.** No `"use client"` module under `src/app/admin/desk/**` or `src/components/**` may contain a literal `/admin/desk…` href, a `router.push`/`replace` target built from one, or a route key string. Every href a client file needs — the roster row link, the wizard's step URLs, the `{field, href}` refusal pushes, the `?reverify=1` target, the tab `?tab=` links — arrives as a string prop from the section's server components, built by 319's one module. The same applies to the bell and email hrefs of PLAN §7. The section's client file names, component names and prop names carry no house word (307, 401).

**Why.** Measured on a real build: a client chunk carries `createServerReference)("4005646da54306e33583d45321c45b2359ece36ec3", u.callServer, void 0, u.findSourceMapURL, "updateGlobalConfigAction")`, so an imported server-action export NAME ships verbatim, and by the same mechanics so does any string literal in a client module; `ROUTE_KEYS` shipping verbatim is the same fact for route strings. With a neutral segment an href literal is no longer a D19 leak by itself, but props-from-the-server is what keeps the segment renameable in ONE place and keeps the section's client files provably word-free for the disclosure walker, whose population is derived from the import graph rather than a list.

#### 331. Neither absence guard is narrowed for this route

**Decision.** `scripts/lib/house-bot-vocabulary.mjs` gains no word, no benign sample and no exemption for Commit 7's routes; `needles` in `scripts/house-bot-console-probe.mts` keeps its one entitled neutralisation (the worktree path) exactly as it is and gains nothing; `verify-house-bot-bundle.mjs` keeps its path-and-content scan unchanged. The probe picks up the three new page instances automatically — it enumerates every `page.tsx` under `src/app/admin` from disk and fills each dynamic segment from the fixture, so the detail route is requested with a real id by a player, the holder and a trigger player in all three modes, and only response BODIES are scanned (`Location` is read to follow an `_rsc` 307 but never scanned). If any non-staff response carries the requested id, the fix is in the RESPONSE — never by teaching `needles` to ignore an id it asked for. ⛔ RECORDED AS EXPECTED, NOT UNMEASURED: on current code such a hit is DERIVABLE, because `AdminSectionGate` titles its restricted panel from the last URL segment and `looksLikeId` keeps an `hb_` + 24-hex segment verbatim ✔ — so 301's optional neutral `title` is a PRECONDITION of this ruling's green, not an independent nicety.

#### 332. A holder who is also an ADMIN stays inside the console's audience, and that is recorded rather than engineered around (X13)

**Decision.** The console's audience is the stored role and nothing else: an ADMIN who also HOLDS a live bot sees the console and its rows. ⛔ No holder-exclusion clause is added to `houseConsoleAudience`, because on a one-owner platform it would lock Ali out of his own console the moment he designated his own account, and because a "not a holder" test would need the holder set read on every gate call — a second read whose failure mode is a locked-out owner. The controls that make this acceptable are named, not assumed: designation REFUSES a staff account outright (`eligibility.ts:248`), so the combination can only arise by PROMOTING an existing holder, which is an owner's own deliberate act; and every ADMIN is already told about every bot by `houseBotAlertRecipients`, so the console discloses nothing the bell does not. **This closes REPLAN ruling 272's X13 note ("a holder with a staff role still sees the console and the house audit rows through 259/260's staff audience — Commit 7's rulings") with the answer: accepted, on the record, with Ali's name on §7 Q7 if he wants the opposite.**

**Proof.** `test:house-bot-console` 1.332: designating an account that holds a staff role is refused (the existing eligibility case), and `houseConsoleAudience(<an ADMIN who holds a live bot>, "/admin/desk")` is true — asserted deliberately, so a future silent holder-exclusion turns this case red and has to be ruled rather than slipped in.

#### 333. The console's nav item carries no badge, and it appears in the mobile nav like any owner-only item

**Decision.** The `desk` nav item is added with NO `badge` and is given no entry in the sidebar badge source. It appears in `admin-mobile-nav` and the sidebar through the same `filterNavGroups` result as every other `ownerOnly` item, with no special case. ⛔ A badge here would be a count of house activity rendered in the console's chrome — outside 266's "used X of Y" and outside D20's list of what a control needs — and the chrome is shared by 47 routes, so a house figure in it is the widest possible placement for the narrowest possible audience.

### Area 3 — the reads and the DAL, rulings 340–359

#### 340. One door, and it is QUERY-shaped: the console's house reads live INSIDE `house-console-read.ts`, and a page never names a house read module

**Decision.** Every server read that `/admin/desk`, `/admin/desk/new` and `/admin/desk/[id]` (and their loaders, route handlers and actions) perform goes through `src/lib/server/house-console-read.ts`. Commit 7 adds to that module a NAMED READER PER SURFACE, each with the gate's own signature `(viewerUserId, route, …payload)`: `houseRosterForConsole(viewer, route)` (control row + roster + the band's two money reads + per-bot wallet), `houseUsageForConsole(viewer, route, query)` (372), `houseEngineForConsole(viewer, route)` (309), `houseFeedForConsole(viewer, route, filter)` (345), `houseEventsForConsole(viewer, route, page)` (317), `houseDetailForConsole(viewer, route, botId)` (358), `houseCheckForConsole(viewer, route, userId)` (359). Each resolves the audience through `houseConsoleAudience` FIRST, performs the reads itself, and returns a PAINTED view model (finished strings, numbers, booleans) or `null`; `null` means the page paints the restricted panel before computing or fetching anything. ⛔ It is NOT thunk-shaped and the route is NOT derived from a header: the gate-call pin fixes the arity, requires arg 0 to be the signed-in session's id and arg 1 to be a STRING LITERAL equal to the file's own console route ✔, so a `(read: () => Promise<T>)` door with no viewer and no route could not be called at all. ⛔ A console page may not import `src/lib/server/house-bot/*`, `src/lib/server/house-bot-dal`, `book.ts` or `cap-precheck.ts` — measured ✔, `CONSOLE_HOUSE_READ_ALLOWED` holds exactly ONE file/module pair (`src/app/admin/system/page.tsx` → `engine-health`) under the docblock "an allowance nothing needs any more is removed, never left standing", and the pin reports an import, a re-export and a kept `import()` alike. **SAME COMMIT, or the law stops covering the new readers — and one clause of 0.260.1 goes RED at step 1 unless it is changed with the code (measured by the completeness review, `scripts/lib/house-bot-reports-cases.mts:1556-1557` ✔):** today that case asserts `r.consoleGateCalls.houseAuditForConsole >= 17` **and `r.consoleGateCalls.houseConsoleAudience === 0`**. Step 1's first gate call breaks the second clause. So in the same commit: `houseConsoleAudience`'s expectation becomes a FLOOR at the count this commit measures (one per page, route handler and action — `>= N`, never a loose `>= 0` and never deleted), the `houseAuditForConsole >= 17` floor is left exactly as it is (a floor is raised only by the session that measures a higher number; it is never lowered), `houseStakeForConsole === 0` and `houseBotLabelsForConsole === 0` stay at zero, and each new export is added to `CONSOLE_GATES` with its arity so the same pin measures it; `house-console-read.ts`'s own header ("what this module gates is the AUDIT ROWS a console page renders, and nothing else") is rewritten to name the readers. The existing `houseAuditForConsole` promise form is unchanged and is used by no house-bot console file (the console reads `HouseBot*` tables, not the platform audit ring).

#### 344. No total is ever built from a paged reader; every count names its counting reader

**Decision.** Every number the console renders that describes a POPULATION comes from a reader whose job is to count — never `rows.length`, never a page's `nextCursor`, never a paged read with a large `limit`. The complete list: roster row count and "Bots N of M" → the length of the ONE `listNonRemoved()` read plus `control.maxDesignatedBots` (346); "Roster full (N of M)" → the same list; the activity badge and the feed's pager total → `countFeed(filter)` (345); the history pager total → `countAll` (317); "Bets today" per bot → `houseDayBooks(day)`'s `bets`; skipped today → `countFeed({statuses:["SKIPPED"], …})`; the unset-limit counts (306, 312) → folded in JS from the already-read control row over `LIMIT_FIELDS`/`CAP_FIELDS`, with NO query; targets → `targetStore.countActive({ botId })`; per-bot bets this hour/day → `botRateUsage` (351).

**Why.** The House DAL clamps every page to 500 and returns `n + 1` rows to decide `nextCursor` ✔, so a paged reader structurally CANNOT report a population; a caller passing `limit: 100_000` gets 500 rows and a confident wrong total. `Pagination` takes `total` as required ✔, so a wrong total is a pager that links to pages which do not exist. Unset-cap counts take no query because the control row is already read for the forms, and a count query there would be a second population that could disagree with the form on the same screen.

#### 347. The roster's per-bot money is ONE `houseDayBooks(dayKey)` plus ONE `openExposure(null)`, summed in JS for the band

**Decision.** Per-bot "Bets today", per-bot open exposure and the band's exposure figure come from a SINGLE `houseDayBooks(dayKey)` (one query, every bot) and a SINGLE `houseBookStore.openExposure(null)` (one query, per-bot rows), both inside `houseRosterForConsole`. The page never reads per row. The band's figures are the JS SUM of the SAME rows the columns render — never a second `houseOpenExposure(null)` and never a second `houseDayBook(day, null)`. Under 266 every one of those figures is rendered as USAGE against its cap (303, 373), never bare. A bot absent from the `houseDayBooks` map renders zeros for today (documented behaviour), which is NOT a failed read (355).

**Why.** `houseDayBooks` exists for this shape — "Every bot's book for one EAT day, in one query (A24). A bot with no stakes that day is absent." — and `openExposure(null)` already returns the per-bot rows `houseOpenExposure` merely sums. Reading the total separately from the rows is the identity-breaking pattern: a stake settling between the two statements makes the band disagree with the column beneath it, and each figure is individually correct, so nothing goes red.

#### 348. Cap usage reads what the GATE for that cap reads, term for term, and the EAT day key is derived ONCE per render from the seam's clock

**Decision.** Every "used X of Y" reads the figure its own gate reads, and the Proof names the RIGHT gate per cap: (i) the money caps — daily stake, projected loss, open exposure, the global four, staff-chosen, balance-vs-floor — are asserted equal to `loadCapFacts`' corresponding `CapFacts` field for the same bot and day; (ii) the REALISED-loss bar reads `houseDayBook(dayKey, botId).realisedLossTzs` and is asserted against the STOP that fires on realised loss, NOT against `loadCapFacts`, which carries NO realised-loss field (its `capDailyLossTzs` test is projected only); (iii) bets per minute and per day for all bots read `houseSeamStore.globalUsage()` and are asserted against the seam's own refusal terms, because `cap-precheck.ts:13-14` EXCLUDES the rate caps on purpose; (iv) per-bot bets this hour/day read 351's `botRateUsage`, asserted equal to `botUsage(...).placedLastHour/placedLastDay`. The day key is computed ONCE per render as `eatDayKey(Date.now())` through `@/lib/house-bot/clock` — the SEAM's derivation, not `dbClock()` — and passed to every `houseDayBook*` call in that render. A NULL cap renders "Not set" with no bar and the consequence 364 assigns it, never a bar at 0%.

**Why.** A usage bar that disagrees with the gate tells the owner a stake will be accepted when it will be refused — the class C4 ruling 153 and L14 established as un-buildable. `book.ts`'s header states the contract: every cap, stop and report that talks about a bot's money reads it there, "so a gate and the console can never disagree about the same day". The clock choice is measured: the seam derives its daily window with `eatDayKey(Date.now())` while the planner uses `dbClock()`; the console must match the GATE that refuses, and deriving it once per render keeps the roster, the band and the limits tab from straddling EAT midnight inside one page. Recorded deviation from 03 §1's "EAT-day logic uses `eat-day.ts` and `lib/query/windows.ts`": the cap cohort is the house EAT PLACEMENT day (R3), not a `FULL_PRESETS` window, and `clock.ts` re-exports `eatDayKey` so there is still one implementation.

#### 353. The stale verdict is the PLANNER beat alone, against `ENGINE_STALE_MS` with `BOOT_GRACE_MS`

**Decision.** ONE server-side predicate, living in `src/lib/server/house-bot/engine-health.ts` and never in a page or a client module, answers "is the engine stale?", and it answers YES only when: the master switch is ON, AND the newest `engine:%` row's `bootAt` is more than `BOOT_GRACE_MS` old (or there is no such row), AND `beat:planner`'s `beatAt` is missing or more than `ENGINE_STALE_MS` old. The `beat:poller:%` beat is rendered as INFORMATION beside it (with `pollerErrorStreak`) and never contributes to the verdict. The verdict is computed on the server and refreshed by the strip's poller; no threshold constant, no `RUNTIME_KEY` and no house-named prop crosses into a client chunk. Recorded consequence: the Callout can appear up to `LIVE_ROUND_MS` (20 s) after the beat goes stale.

**Why.** Measured, and it REVERSES `PLAN.md:450`'s OR. The poller writes its beat ONLY after a claim — `worker.ts:26` returns BEFORE the beat when it claimed nothing, and the file's header says so: "⛔ A BEAT ONLY AFTER A CLAIM (A24) … A pass that claimed nothing writes nothing." So a perfectly healthy engine with nothing due writes no poller beat, and PLAN's OR would paint "The bot engine is not running. No bot will place a bet." on an idle, healthy engine — a false alarm on the console's gravest statement, and an alarm that is usually wrong is one nobody reads. The planner beat IS liveness: it is written whenever the money-safety duties succeed, work or no work, and `PLANNER_INTERVAL_MS` 15 s against `ENGINE_STALE_MS` 30 s leaves exactly one missed tick of headroom. The boot grace is not optional: `FIRST_TICK_DELAY_MS` is 20 s, so without `BOOT_GRACE_MS` 90 s every deploy paints the danger Callout. Measured too: both constants have NO importer anywhere in `src/` or `scripts/` — they were declared for this console, so Commit 7 writes the only predicate and writes it once.

**Files.** `src/lib/house-bot/constants.ts:551`, `:554`, `:555`, `:556` · `src/lib/server/house-bot/worker.ts:8`, `:26`, `:29` · `src/lib/server/house-bot/planner.ts:215`, `:217` · `src/lib/refresh-cadence.ts:46` · `src/lib/server/house-bot/status-display.ts:9` (the precedent for keeping a verdict server-side).

**Proof.** `test:house-bot-engine`, both stores: (1) switch ON, boot 10 min ago, planner beat 45 s old, NO poller beat at all → STALE; (2) the same with the planner beat 10 s old and no poller beat → NOT stale (the idle-engine false alarm, red under PLAN's OR); (3) switch ON, boot 30 s ago, no beats → NOT stale, and at boot + 91 s with no planner beat → STALE; (4) switch OFF with no beats → NOT stale. Controls: a predicate that ORs the poller beat makes (2) red; one that drops the boot grace makes (3) red. Plus `verify:house-bot-bundle` and a source case that no `"use client"` file in the house-bot graph imports `@/lib/house-bot/constants`.

#### 354. A failed health read paints the danger Callout; and the gate never turns a failure into a false VERDICT

**Decision.** (a) `houseEngineHealthFor` is made to fail closed on the viewer lookup the way `houseConsoleAudience` already does — `return null` rather than throw — so the two gates cannot disagree about a transient failure, and `/admin/system`'s `.catch(() => null)` becomes redundant and is removed. (b) The console's readers distinguish their two null causes IN THEIR RETURN, not by inference: `{ verdict: "outside" }` when the stored role was read and is outside the audience, and `{ verdict: "unreadable" }` when the viewer lookup itself failed. Both refuse the read and both are fail-closed, but only "outside" paints the restricted panel; "unreadable" paints a neutral panel saying the page could not decide who is viewing, naming no house word — the only honest thing to show an OWNER whose database is unreachable, instead of "Your role cannot view this page", which would be a false statement about his own role during an outage. (c) Inside the audience, a failed health read renders the S5 Callout with `meta` "Last seen: unknown" — never an absent card and never a healthy-looking one.

**Why.** 03 S5 is explicit: "A failed beat read renders the same Callout with the meta 'Last seen: unknown'. An unreadable health state must not render as healthy (§C2)." The current pair does the opposite: `engine-health.ts:26-27` places `db.user.findById` OUTSIDE the `try` that begins at `:29`, so a pool timeout propagates; `/admin/system:189`'s catch turns it into the `null` that means "not in the audience"; and `:482`'s `{houseEngine && …}` renders nothing. On the console that would be a page that looks healthy while nobody could tell — Ali's own "'Not applicable' is the most dangerous silent verdict", in its exact form.

#### 355. The failed-read path is decided per read, the reads are SETTLED, and a failed COUNT renders `AdminLoadError`

**Decision.** Inside each gated reader every read is issued as a SETTLED set (`Promise.allSettled`), so no single failure blanks the page and each failure is attributed to ITS figure: a failed roster read → `AdminLoadError what="the roster"`, never `AdminTableEmpty`; a failed money read → the KPI's `unavailable` and the cell "—", never 0 and never a bar at 0%; a failed cap read → no bar and a sentence saying the usage could not be read, never "used 0 of Y"; a failed health read → 354's Callout; a failed TAB COUNT → that tab's panel renders `AdminLoadError`, because `CountBadge` renders nothing at zero and a failed count is otherwise indistinguishable from an empty tab; a failed wallet read (the detail page's one, or the wizard's one — the roster has none, 356) → the floor sentence is replaced by "the balance floor could not be checked", never "above the floor" and never an amount; a `HouseSchemaNotReady` from the control read → 421's neutral schema Callout, which is a STATE and not a failure. Consequently the readers do not REJECT for these reads, and 340's rejection path exists only for a throw nobody anticipated, which the page's catch renders as 354(b)'s undecidable panel — never as a stack trace, because a house error's stack is itself a house value the vocabulary matches.

Plus a pin that the readers use a settling combinator and not `Promise.all`. `red:house-bot-console` carries the eight mutations.

#### 356. No house read is cached across requests, and the per-row wallet reads are bounded and isolated

**Decision.** Every console route sets `export const dynamic = "force-dynamic"`, and no house read is wrapped in `unstable_cache`, given a `revalidate`, or memoised anywhere but React's per-render-pass `cache()` (342). ⛔ **THE ROSTER PERFORMS NO WALLET READ AT ALL — corrected by the completeness review, and it removes a read from step 1.** The draft had `houseRosterForConsole` calling `db.wallet.findByUserId` once per rendered row; measured against 310 and 368, NOTHING RENDERS IT: the roster's eight columns carry no balance and no floor state (310), and 368 strikes the balance from the bot page too. A per-row holder wallet read whose value is never painted is up to twenty players' live balances pulled into a render on a route 259 streams to any signed-in account — the exact harm 368 argues from, taken for nothing. So: the roster reader reads no wallet; `houseDetailForConsole` reads the ONE holder's wallet for 368's floor STATE sentence (one read, never an amount on the page); `houseCheckForConsole` reads the ONE candidate's wallet for the wizard's single plain balance (368's stated exception); a REMOVED bot's page reads no wallet at all (358). `wallet.listAll` is never called anywhere in the console, and no console surface reads a wallet it does not paint. **READ-TIERS, decided on the measured facts:** `money.figures` IS in `MASKABLE_CLASSES` ✔ and READ-TIERS §4c makes a `read` cell "masked AT REST, and this role may REVEAL it (audited)" — but masking is realised per FIELD through `SENSITIVE_FIELDS`, whose entries are exactly `email`, `phone`, `msisdn`, `region`, `dob` ✔ with no money field, so there is no masked form to rest in and no reveal to audit, and ADMIN's `money.figures` cell is `read` ✔. The console therefore renders its cap figures in full for the Owner, renders NO `SENSITIVE_FIELDS`-registered field at all (346's `playerHandle`), and keeps the ONE field that IS registered behind the platform's own control: the wizard's check card renders the holder's phone through the SERVER `Sensitive` component, which is what calls `readCell` (359, 412). ⛔ If a money field is ever wired into `SENSITIVE_FIELDS`, this ruling is re-opened rather than exempted.

#### 358. A REMOVED bot's page is read-only, and its read set is named

**Decision.** `/admin/desk/[id]` is served by `houseDetailForConsole(viewer, "/admin/desk", id)`, which reads the bot with `houseBotStore.get(id)` — NOT `listNonRemoved` — so a REMOVED bot renders, as PLAN §8 requires. For `status === "REMOVED"` the reader returns: the bot row, its final status and reason, its saved rules, its history page (317) and its activity page (345) — and NOTHING ELSE: no cap usage (there is nothing left to use), no engine health, no wallet read, no targets, no rate usage. The page renders those, a `neutral` Callout stating the terminal state, and NO action buttons. `notFound()` is reserved for an id no row matches (302), after the audience verdict (399).

**Why.** `listNonRemoved` excludes a removed bot in both twins ✔, so without this the read-only state PLAN promises is unreachable and the page would 404 on a row that exists. Reading caps and health for a bot that can never bet again would be a set of figures with no control attached, which is what D20 removed.

**Proof.** `test:house-bot-console` 1.358: a REMOVED bot's page renders its status, reason, rules, history and activity, renders no usage bar, no engine Callout and no action button, and a spy records ZERO wallet, cap, rate and target reads for it; an unknown id 404s for the ADMIN and answers identically to a real id for a player.

#### 359. The wizard's check card reads through the gate, not through `eligibility.ts`

**Decision.** `/admin/desk/new` is served by `houseCheckForConsole(viewer, "/admin/desk", userId)`, which inside the gate module calls `houseBotEligibility(...)` and returns a PAINTED check card: the blocking and warning rows as finished sentences (`eligibility.ts` already builds them, money included — role D of 360), the holder's handle, the live balance figure (the one balance the console shows, 368), the bonus line, the open-position count, and the phone. ⛔ **THE PHONE'S SHAPE IS CORRECTED by the completeness review, because the draft's shape SKIPPED the gate.** `SensitiveReveal` (`src/components/ui/sensitive-reveal.tsx:25` ✔) is the CLIENT control; the gate is the SERVER component `Sensitive` (`src/components/ui/sensitive.tsx:52` ✔), which resolves the viewer's stored role through its own per-pass `cache()` (`:46-50` ✔), calls `readCell(role, spec.readClass)` (`:74` ✔), renders NOTHING for `none` (`:75`), renders the masked span for `masked` (`:82-83`) and only then renders `SensitiveReveal` (`:87`). A console page handed a `{ field, subjectId, masked }` triple and rendering `SensitiveReveal` itself would bypass `readCell` entirely — READ-TIERS answered in a `.tsx`, which `sensitive.tsx:40-43` forbids in as many words. So the reader returns `{ holderUserId, phoneE164 }` as SERVER-ONLY fields and the wizard's own SERVER component renders `<Sensitive field="phone" subjectId={holderUserId} value={phoneE164} domainAllows />`; no console file imports `SensitiveReveal`, builds a masked value, or calls `spec.mask`. The raw value never reaches a client prop — `Sensitive` computes the mask and the client receives the mask only (`:61-62`, `:77`). ⛔ The wizard page may NOT import `eligibility.ts` or `designation.ts`: both are under `src/lib/server/house-bot/`, and `CONSOLE_HOUSE_READ_ALLOWED` holds one unrelated pair ✔. The reader also returns 346's prior-designation history from `listByUserId`.

Plus `test:read-tiers` re-run (356).

### Area 4 — money as usage only, rulings 360–375

#### 360. Money appears in four roles only, in rendered text AND in any attribute

**Decision.** A figure denominated in shillings may appear on a Commit 7 console surface, in rendered text OR in any attribute the viewer's DOM receives (`title`, `aria-label`, `aria-valuetext`), only in one of these roles: **(A)** a configured limit's own value, in the control that edits it (the rules and limits inputs, and the "Use recommended values" figures from `FIELD_META.recommended`); **(B)** usage of ONE configured limit rendered together with that limit, in 361's fixed form — per bot: daily stake used of `capDailyStakeTzs`, projected loss and settled loss used of `capDailyLossTzs`, open exposure used of `capOpenExposureTzs`, staff-chosen stake used of `capStaffChosenDailyTzs`; all bots: the same five against `gCapDailyStakeTzs`, `gCapDailyLossTzs` (twice), `gCapOpenExposureTzs`, `gCapStaffChosenDailyTzs`; **(C)** the amount of ONE action the officer is looking at or about to take — one intent's `stakeTzs` in the feed, the Enter-now preview's asked stake and the cut it was reduced to, a cancelled intent's stake — never summed, never netted, never carried forward; **(D)** a refusal's or auto-pause's own reason naming the cap it hit and the figure that hit it, as `eligibility.ts`, `pause-reasons.ts` and `alert-copy.ts` already build them. Struck and never built: any net, profit, return, won/lost, fee-withheld, lifetime or month figure; any "Book" card; any signed money; the holder's balance on a running bot's page (368); the holder's transaction list (369); any per-market, per-player or per-officer money (365). **Two consequences stated because the drafts left holes:** `gCapPerMarketTzs` and `gCounterPerPlayerTzsPerDay` are in `REQUIRED_FOR_MASTER_ON` but carry no usage row under 365, so their unset-blocks-ON consequence is stated on the FORM FIELD (364), never as a usage figure; and role C and D strings arrive ALREADY BUILT by their own server module and are rendered verbatim, so 361's formatter law governs only figures the console itself formats. Platform money that main's own surfaces already show for any market or player is not house money and this ruling does not reach it.

⛔ **ONE CARVE-OUT, NAMED, BECAUSE THE KIT WRITES AN ATTRIBUTE THE CONSOLE CANNOT REMOVE (found by the completeness review).** `AdminKpi` sets `title={typeof value === "string" ? value : undefined}` on its value element (`src/components/admin/admin-shell.tsx:497-501` ✔) — so every money tile 404 permits automatically ships a BARE amount in a `title`, with its limit in the delta and not in the title. Removing it means forking the kit, which §K5 forbids, and the attribute is the kit's own overflow affordance (the value carries `truncate`). So the attribute rule is: a money attribute is permitted where it is the KIT's own mirror of a figure already permitted in that element's text — the `AdminKpi` value `title` and `ProgressBar`'s `aria-valuetext`, which 362 fills from `captionText` — and nowhere else. 1.360's walk therefore reports a `title`/`aria-label`/`aria-valuetext` carrying a digit group at a CONSOLE call site, and does not report the two kit-internal mirrors; its control plants a money `title` written by the console (on a `<td>`, a `<span>` or a `<Button>`) and requires it to be reported, so the carve-out cannot swallow the rule.

**Why.** Ruling 266 gives the form and three examples but not the closed set, and D20's default is no results report, CSV or per-market house line beyond what a control needs. Without a closed set the console re-acquires the struck book one honest-looking figure at a time — `houseBotBook` still ships `netTzs` today, which is exactly the "Today's net" 266 struck. Roles A–D are each something a control cannot work without: you cannot edit a cap you cannot see, judge a cap without its usage, approve a stake whose amount is hidden, or act on a refusal that does not name its figure (§F4).
